<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> A countable discrete group is amenable if and only if it is strongly Ulam stable: every sufficiently accurate unitary almost representation, on any complex Hilbert space, is uniformly close in operator norm to a genuine representation on the same space. We prove the converse to Kazhdan's amenable stability theorem, answering the question of Burger, Ozawa, and Thom. The inclusion of infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces is essential.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): A countable discrete group is strongly Ulam stable if and only if it is amenable.
  - Explanation: Characterizes amenable countable discrete groups by strong Ulam stability, establishing that every sufficiently accurate unitary almost representation on any complex Hilbert space is uniformly close in operator norm to a genuine unitary representation on the same space.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-list)): Let $K$ be a compact Hausdorff boundary, let $U\subseteq K$ be nonempty and open, let $\zeta\in K$, and let $0<\beta<1$. There is a finite nonempty list $s_1,\ldots,s_M\in G$, with repetitions allowed, such that $$s_j^{-1}\zeta\in U\quad(1\leq j\leq M), \qquad \frac{1}{M}\#\{j:s_j^{-1}t\notin U\}<\beta \quad(t\in K).$$ Moreover, some open neighborhood $O$ of $\zeta$ satisfies $s_j^{-1}O\subseteq U$ for every $j$.
  - Explanation: Uses boundary actions and strong proximality to construct finite lists of group elements that map a distinguished point into a specified open set while mapping all boundary points into that set with failure frequency below beta.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-flags)): Let $G$ be a nonamenable discrete group. For every integer $N\geq1$ and every $0<\beta<1$, there are finite nonempty sets $\mathcal X_1,\ldots,\mathcal X_N$ and $\mathcal Y_0,\ldots,\mathcal Y_{N-1}$, maps $$s_k:\mathcal X_k\times\mathcal Y_{k-1}\longrightarrow G, \qquad b_k:G\times\mathcal X_k\times\mathcal Y_{k-1} \longrightarrow\{\mathsf E,\mathsf F\},$$ with the following properties.* 1. *If $k<N$ and $b_k(w;x,y)=\mathsf E$, then, with $v=w s_k(x,y)$, $$b_{k+1}(v;x',y')=\mathsf E \quad\text{for every }(x',y')\in \mathcal X_{k+1}\times\mathcal Y_k.$$* 2. *For every $k,w,x$, $$\frac{\#\{y\in\mathcal Y_{k-1}:b_k(w;x,y)=\mathsf F\}} {\|\mathcal Y_{k-1}\|}\leq\beta.$$* 3. *For every $k,v,y$, $$\frac{\#\{x\in\mathcal X_k: b_k(v s_k(x,y)^{-1};x,y)=\mathsf E\}} {\|\mathcal X_k\|}\leq\beta.$$* *Each alphabet may subsequently be replaced by any positive integer number of copies, independently of the others, while preserving these properties. In particular, its cardinality can be made arbitrarily large and divisible by any prescribed positive integer.
  - Explanation: Constructs multi-stage alphabets and transition flags such that an E flag prevents subsequent F flags, F flags are rare along departures, and E flags are rare along arrivals.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse)): For every $0<\eta<1/4$ there is $0<\beta_0<1/16$ with the following property. If $0<\beta\leq\beta_0$, then for every sufficiently large integer $m$ there are unit vectors $(a_x)_{x\in X}$ in $\mathbb R^m$, where $\|X\|=2m$, and signs $f(x)\in\{1,-1\}$ such that:* 1. *the vectors with either sign form an orthonormal basis, and hence $$\label{eq:frame-cancellation} \sum_{x\in X} f(x)a_xa_x^*=0;$$* 2. *if $S\subseteq X$ and $\|S\|\leq4\beta\|X\|$, the synthesis map $F_S:\ell^2(S)\to\mathbb C^m$, $F_Se_x=a_x$, satisfies $$\label{eq:sparse-gram} \left\lVert F_S^*F_S-I\right\rVert\leq\eta.$$* *Moreover, for every sufficiently large integer $n$ there is a real orthogonal $n\times n$ matrix $O$ such that, on a set $Y$ of size $2n$, $$\label{eq:JZ} J=\begin{pmatrix}0&O\\ O^*&0\end{pmatrix}, \qquad Z=\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\0&-I\end{pmatrix}$$ satisfy $$\label{eq:sparse-J} \left\lVert P_SJP_T\right\rVert\leq\eta \quad\text{whenever}\quad \|S\|,\|T\|\leq4\beta\|Y\|.$$ In particular, $J^*=J$, $J^2=Z^2=I$, $JZ=-ZJ$, and every diagonal entry of $J$ is zero.
  - Explanation: Constructs signed pairs of orthonormal bases with cancellation whose synthesis maps are almost isometric on sparse label subsets, alongside anticommuting symmetric involutions with sparse restrictions bounded by eta.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-priority)): Let $A,B$ be positive operators on a Hilbert space $E$ such that $$\tfrac12I\leq A,B\leq2I, \qquad A-B=h\Gamma, \qquad \Gamma^*=\Gamma, \qquad \left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert\leq1, \qquad 0\leq h\leq\tfrac18.$$ Let $P$ be an orthogonal projection commuting with $B$, set $R=PE$, and assume $\left\lVert P\Gamma P\right\rVert\leq\eta$, where $0\leq\eta\leq1$. There is a specified invertible operator $S_R$ such that $$\label{eq:priority-estimates} S_R^*AS_R=B,\qquad S_R(R)=R,\qquad \left\lVert S_R-I\right\rVert\leq Ch,\qquad \left\lVert(S_R-I)P\right\rVert\leq Ch\eta.$$ The constant $C$ is absolute, and the estimates also hold in the $B$-norm after changing $C$.* *If $R\subseteq R'$ and both projections satisfy these hypotheses, then $$\label{eq:nested-transfer} K=S_{R'}^{-1}S_R \quad\text{satisfies}\quad K^*BK=B,\qquad \left\lVert K-I\right\rVert_B\leq Ch,\qquad \left\lVert(K-I)P\right\rVert_B\leq Ch\eta.$$ The construction commutes with orthogonal direct sums and with every orthogonal projection commuting with $A$, $B$, and $P$.
  - Explanation: Constructs an invertible transfer operator between two close positive metrics that preserves a designated priority subspace and acts on it with an error controlled by the compressed difference.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-truncate)): Let $U$ be a unitary on a Hilbert space $E$, and let $P$ be an orthogonal projection. Suppose $$\left\lVert U-I\right\rVert\leq t<\tfrac12, \qquad \left\lVert(U-I)P\right\rVert\leq e\leq\tfrac12.$$ There is a unitary $\widehat U$ that is the identity on $PE$ and leaves $(I-P)E$ invariant, with $$\left\lVert\widehat U-U\right\rVert\leq3e, \qquad \left\lVert\widehat U-I\right\rVert\leq t+3e.$$ The statement holds in arbitrary Hilbert space dimension.
  - Explanation: Modifies a unitary operator that approximately fixes a subspace into an exact unitary that acts as the identity on that subspace with dimension-free error bounds.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-comparison)): There are numerical constants $C$ and $h_0>0$ such that, for the construction of Section <a href="#sec:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:construction">4</a> with $h\le h_0$ and $0<\eta\le1/4$, $$\label{eq:comparison} \left\lVert V_{qp}^{\{p,q\}}-V_{qp}^{\{p,q,r\}}\right\rVert \le Ch+CN\eta+CNh\eta$$ for every three configurations $p,q,r$. The constants do not depend on the cardinalities of any alphabets or on the configurations.
  - Explanation: Establishes a dimension-free bound on the operator-norm difference between pairwise and triple configuration circuit comparisons, preventing error accumulation over multiple levels.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transport)): For all but a proportion $2N\beta$ of labels $i\in\mathcal I$, $$\label{eq:transport-bound} \left\lVert\mu(s_i)u_i-r_i\right\rVert\le CN\eta+CNh\eta.$$
  - Explanation: Shows that on all except a small proportion of label sequences, the almost representation transports the unit vectors at the final level to the initial level within controlled error.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-witness)): Let $\rho:G\to\mathcal U(H)$ be a unitary representation, and define unit vectors in $\mathbb C^{\mathcal I}\otimes H$ by $$\psi=\left\lvert\mathcal I\right\rvert^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I}e_i\otimes\rho(s_i)u_i, \qquad r=\left\lvert\mathcal I\right\rvert^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I}e_i\otimes r_i.$$ Then $$\label{eq:witness-values} \left\langle\psi,(D\otimes I)\psi\right\rangle=0, \qquad \left\langle r,(D\otimes I)r\right\rangle=\frac12.$$ In particular, $\left\lVert r-\psi\right\rVert\ge1/4$.
  - Explanation: Establishes exact expectation values for the witness operator on genuine representation vectors and reference vectors, deducing a universal lower bound on their norm distance.
- **C10** (counterexample, [source](#eq:distance-goal)): More precisely, for every nonamenable countable group and every $\delta>0$, we construct a separable Hilbert space $H$ and a normalized map $\mu:G\to\mathcal U(H)$ with $\operatorname{def}(\mu)<\delta$ such that $$\label{eq:distance-goal} \sup_{g\in G}\left\lVert\mu(g)-\rho(g)\right\rVert>\frac1{10} \quad\text{for every unitary representation }\rho:G\to\mathcal U(H).$$
  - Explanation: Constructs, for every nonamenable countable group and every prescribed positive defect tolerance, a normalized unitary almost representation on a separable Hilbert space that stays bounded away in operator norm from every genuine unitary representation on the same space.

For every integer $N\\geq1$ and every $0<\\beta<1$, there are finite nonempty sets $\\mathcal X_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal X_N$ and $\\mathcal Y_0,\\ldots,\\mathcal Y_{N-1}$, maps $$s_k:\\mathcal X_k\\times\\mathcal Y_{k-1}\\longrightarrow G,\n \\qquad\n b_k:G\\times\\mathcal X_k\\times\\mathcal Y_{k-1}\n       \\longrightarrow\\{\\mathsf E,\\mathsf F\\},$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *If $k<N$ and $b_k(w;x,y)=\\mathsf E$, then, with $v=w s_k(x,y)$, $$b_{k+1}(v;x',y')=\\mathsf E\n     \\quad\\text{for every }(x',y')\\in\n                \\mathcal X_{k+1}\\times\\mathcal Y_k.$$*\n\n2.  *For every $k,w,x$, $$\\frac{\\#\\{y\\in\\mathcal Y_{k-1}:b_k(w;x,y)=\\mathsf F\\}}\n          {|\\mathcal Y_{k-1}|}\\leq\\beta.$$*\n\n3.  *For every $k,v,y$, $$\\frac{\\#\\{x\\in\\mathcal X_k:\n              b_k(v s_k(x,y)^{-1};x,y)=\\mathsf E\\}}\n          {|\\mathcal X_k|}\\leq\\beta.$$*\n\n*Each alphabet may subsequently be replaced by any positive integer number of copies, independently of the others, while preserving these properties. In particular, its cardinality can be made arbitrarily large and divisible by any prescribed positive integer.",
          "source_id": "PM-251.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be a nonamenable discrete group.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sparse",
        "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\eta<1/4$ there is $0<\\beta_0<1/16$ with the following property. If $0<\\beta\\leq\\beta_0$, then for every sufficiently large integer $m$ there are unit vectors $(a_x)_{x\\in X}$ in $\\mathbb R^m$, where $|X|=2m$, and signs $f(x)\\in\\{1,-1\\}$ such that:*\n\n1.  *the vectors with either sign form an orthonormal basis, and hence $$\\label{eq:frame-cancellation}\n     \\sum_{x\\in X} f(x)a_xa_x^*=0;$$*\n\n\n2.  *if $S\\subseteq X$ and $|S|\\leq4\\beta|X|$, the synthesis map $F_S:\\ell^2(S)\\to\\mathbb C^m$, $F_Se_x=a_x$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:sparse-gram}\n     \\left\\lVert F_S^*F_S-I\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta.$$*\n\n\n\n*Moreover, for every sufficiently large integer $n$ there is a real orthogonal $n\\times n$ matrix $O$ such that, on a set $Y$ of size $2n$, $$\\label{eq:JZ}\n J=\\begin{pmatrix}0&O\\\\ O^*&0\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad Z=\\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\\\0&-I\\end{pmatrix}$$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:sparse-J}\n \\left\\lVert P_SJP_T\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta\n \\quad\\text{whenever}\\quad |S|,|T|\\leq4\\beta|Y|.$$ In particular, $J^*=J$, $J^2=Z^2=I$, $JZ=-ZJ$, and every diagonal entry of $J$ is zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-251.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sparse",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-priority",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A,B$ be positive operators on a Hilbert space $E$ such that $$\\tfrac12I\\leq A,B\\leq2I,\n \\qquad A-B=h\\Gamma,\n \\qquad \\Gamma^*=\\Gamma,\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert\\Gamma\\right\\rVert\\leq1,\n \\qquad 0\\leq h\\leq\\tfrac18.$$ Let $P$ be an orthogonal projection commuting with $B$, set $R=PE$, and assume $\\left\\lVert P\\Gamma P\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta$, where $0\\leq\\eta\\leq1$. There is a specified invertible operator $S_R$ such that $$\\label{eq:priority-estimates}\n S_R^*AS_R=B,\\qquad S_R(R)=R,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert S_R-I\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(S_R-I)P\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The constant $C$ is absolute, and the estimates also hold in the $B$-norm after changing $C$.*\n\n\n*If $R\\subseteq R'$ and both projections satisfy these hypotheses, then $$\\label{eq:nested-transfer}\n K=S_{R'}^{-1}S_R\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad\n K^*BK=B,\\qquad \\left\\lVert K-I\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(K-I)P\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The construction commutes with orthogonal direct sums and with every orthogonal projection commuting with $A$, $B$, and $P$.",
        "source_id": "PM-251.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-priority",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A,B$ be positive operators on a Hilbert space $E$ such that $$\\tfrac12I\\leq A,B\\leq2I,\n \\qquad A-B=h\\Gamma,\n \\qquad \\Gamma^*=\\Gamma,\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert\\Gamma\\right\\rVert\\leq1,\n \\qquad 0\\leq h\\leq\\tfrac18.$$ Let $P$ be an orthogonal projection commuting with $B$, set $R=PE$, and assume $\\left\\lVert P\\Gamma P\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta$, where $0\\leq\\eta\\leq1$. There is a specified invertible operator $S_R$ such that $$\\label{eq:priority-estimates}\n S_R^*AS_R=B,\\qquad S_R(R)=R,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert S_R-I\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(S_R-I)P\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The constant $C$ is absolute, and the estimates also hold in the $B$-norm after changing $C$.*\n\n\n*If $R\\subseteq R'$ and both projections satisfy these hypotheses, then $$\\label{eq:nested-transfer}\n K=S_{R'}^{-1}S_R\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad\n K^*BK=B,\\qquad \\left\\lVert K-I\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(K-I)P\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The construction commutes with orthogonal direct sums and with every orthogonal projection commuting with $A$, $B$, and $P$.",
          "source_id": "PM-251.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $A,B$ be positive operators on a Hilbert space $E$ such that $$\\tfrac12I\\leq A,B\\leq2I,\n \\qquad A-B=h\\Gamma,\n \\qquad \\Gamma^*=\\Gamma,\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert\\Gamma\\right\\rVert\\leq1,\n \\qquad 0\\leq h\\leq\\tfrac18.$$ Let $P$ be an orthogonal projection commuting with $B$, set $R=PE$, and assume $\\left\\lVert P\\Gamma P\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta$, where $0\\leq\\eta\\leq1$. There is a specified invertible operator $S_R$ such that $$\\label{eq:priority-estimates}\n S_R^*AS_R=B,\\qquad S_R(R)=R,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert S_R-I\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(S_R-I)P\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The constant $C$ is absolute, and the estimates also hold in the $B$-norm after changing $C$.*\n\n\n*If $R\\subseteq R'$ and both projections satisfy these hypotheses, then $$\\label{eq:nested-transfer}\n K=S_{R'}^{-1}S_R\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad\n K^*BK=B,\\qquad \\left\\lVert K-I\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(K-I)P\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The construction commutes with orthogonal direct sums and with every orthogonal projection commuting with $A$, $B$, and $P$.",
      "explanation": "Constructs an invertible transfer operator between two close positive metrics that preserves a designated priority subspace and acts on it with an error controlled by the compressed difference.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-priority",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A,B$ be positive operators on a Hilbert space $E$ such that $$\\tfrac12I\\leq A,B\\leq2I,\n \\qquad A-B=h\\Gamma,\n \\qquad \\Gamma^*=\\Gamma,\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert\\Gamma\\right\\rVert\\leq1,\n \\qquad 0\\leq h\\leq\\tfrac18.$$ Let $P$ be an orthogonal projection commuting with $B$, set $R=PE$, and assume $\\left\\lVert P\\Gamma P\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta$, where $0\\leq\\eta\\leq1$. There is a specified invertible operator $S_R$ such that $$\\label{eq:priority-estimates}\n S_R^*AS_R=B,\\qquad S_R(R)=R,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert S_R-I\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(S_R-I)P\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The constant $C$ is absolute, and the estimates also hold in the $B$-norm after changing $C$.*\n\n\n*If $R\\subseteq R'$ and both projections satisfy these hypotheses, then $$\\label{eq:nested-transfer}\n K=S_{R'}^{-1}S_R\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad\n K^*BK=B,\\qquad \\left\\lVert K-I\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(K-I)P\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The construction commutes with orthogonal direct sums and with every orthogonal projection commuting with $A$, $B$, and $P$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-truncate",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a unitary on a Hilbert space $E$, and let $P$ be an orthogonal projection. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Almost representation defect** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a map $\mu:G\to\mathcal U(H)$ with $\mu(e)=I$, put $$\operatorname{def}(\mu)=\sup_{g,h\in G}\left\lVert\mu(gh)-\mu(g)\mu(h)\right\rVert.$$
- **Strong Ulam stability** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The group is *strongly Ulam stable* if, for every $\varepsilon>0$, there is $\delta>0$ such that every such map with $\operatorname{def}(\mu)\le\delta$ is within $\varepsilon$, uniformly on $G$, of a unitary representation on the same space $H$.
- **Amenable group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A group is amenable if $\ell^\infty(G)$ admits a positive linear functional $m$ with $m(1)=1$ and $m(L_gf)=m(f)$, where $(L_gf)(x)=f(g^{-1}x)$.
- **Group boundary** ([source](#sec:flags)): A compact $G$-space is a *boundary* if its action is minimal and strongly proximal: every orbit is dense, and the weak-star closure of the orbit of every Radon probability measure contains a point mass.
- **Jordan-Wigner Clifford operators** ([source](#pm-h-9)): On the formal orthonormal label space $\mathbb C^{\mathcal I}$ define $$\label{eq:clifford} \gamma_d=f_d(X_d)Z_1\cdots Z_{d-1}J_d, \qquad D=\frac1{\sqrt L}\sum_{d=1}^L\gamma_d.$$
- **Level metric operator** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Set $$\label{eq:level-metric} G_k=I+h\sum_{k<d\le L}\gamma_d.$$
- **Multi-level Hilbert space** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Take the Hilbert direct sum $$\label{eq:big-space} H=\bigoplus_{k=0}^N\ \bigoplus_{w\in G}(E_k,G_k), \qquad H_k=\bigoplus_{w\in G}(E_k,G_k).$$
- **Stage edge** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A stage-$k$ edge is a quadruple $(w,k,x,y)$, with arrival $v=ws_k(x,y)$.
- **Switchable edge** ([source](#pm-h-10)): It is *switchable for $\mathcal C$* if its flags are not the same in all configurations of $\mathcal C$.
- **Almost representation map** ([source](#eq:mu)): Return to the original configuration $b$ and define $$\label{eq:mu} \mu(g)=V_{b,gb}^{\{b,gb\}}U(g).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | \sup_{g\in G}\left\lVert\mu(g)-\rho(g)\right\rVert | > | $\frac1{10}$ | Every unitary representation on the constructed separable Hilbert space. |
| Q2 | \frac{1}{M}\#\{j:s_j^{-1}t\notin U\} | < | $\beta$ | All points in the compact Hausdorff boundary. |
| Q3 | \beta_0 | < | $1/16$ | Admissible sparsity threshold in the sparse restriction lemma. |
| Q4 | \left\lVert F_S^*F_S-I\right\rVert | ≤ | $\eta$ | Synthesis map of any sparse subset of the signed union of two orthonormal bases. |
| Q5 | h | ≤ | $\tfrac18$ | Admissible metric step perturbation parameter for subspace-preserving transfer. |
| Q6 | \left\lVert\widehat U-U\right\rVert | ≤ | $3e$ | Operator norm distance between the modified subspace-fixing unitary and the original unitary. |
| Q7 | h | = | $\frac1{2\sqrt L}$ | Step size scaling factor for the level metric perturbations. |
| Q8 | G_k | ≤ | $\frac32 I$ | Level metric operator at every level k from 0 to N. |
| Q9 | \left\lVert V_{qp}^{\{p,q\}}-V_{qp}^{\{p,q,r\}}\right\rVert | ≤ | $Ch+CN\eta+CNh\eta$ | Every triple of translated flag configurations across arbitrary alphabet cardinalities. |
| Q10 | \operatorname{def}(\mu) | ≤ | $Ch+CN\eta+CNh\eta$ | Uniform operator-norm multiplicativity defect of the constructed almost representation. |
| Q11 | \left\lVert\mu(s_i)u_i-r_i\right\rVert | ≤ | $CN\eta+CNh\eta$ | Norm transport error between final-level and initial-level unit label vectors. |
| Q12 | \left\lVert r-\psi\right\rVert | ≥ | $1/4$ | Euclidean norm distance between the reference state vector and the representation state vector. |

If $0<\\beta\\leq\\beta_0$, then for every sufficiently large integer $m$ there are unit vectors $(a_x)_{x\\in X}$ in $\\mathbb R^m$, where $|X|=2m$, and signs $f(x)\\in\\{1,-1\\}$ such that:*\n\n1.  *the vectors with either sign form an orthonormal basis, and hence $$\\label{eq:frame-cancellation}\n     \\sum_{x\\in X} f(x)a_xa_x^*=0;$$*\n\n\n2.  *if $S\\subseteq X$ and $|S|\\leq4\\beta|X|$, the synthesis map $F_S:\\ell^2(S)\\to\\mathbb C^m$, $F_Se_x=a_x$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:sparse-gram}\n     \\left\\lVert F_S^*F_S-I\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta.$$*\n\n\n\n*Moreover, for every sufficiently large integer $n$ there is a real orthogonal $n\\times n$ matrix $O$ such that, on a set $Y$ of size $2n$, $$\\label{eq:JZ}\n J=\\begin{pmatrix}0&O\\\\ O^*&0\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad Z=\\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\\\0&-I\\end{pmatrix}$$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:sparse-J}\n \\left\\lVert P_SJP_T\\right\\rVert\\leq\\eta\n \\quad\\text{whenever}\\quad |S|,|T|\\leq4\\beta|Y|.$$ In particular, $J^*=J$, $J^2=Z^2=I$, $JZ=-ZJ$, and every diagonal entry of $J$ is zero.",
            "source_id": "PM-251.2"
          }
        ],

There is a specified invertible operator $S_R$ such that $$\\label{eq:priority-estimates}\n S_R^*AS_R=B,\\qquad S_R(R)=R,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert S_R-I\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(S_R-I)P\\right\\rVert\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The constant $C$ is absolute, and the estimates also hold in the $B$-norm after changing $C$.*\n\n\n*If $R\\subseteq R'$ and both projections satisfy these hypotheses, then $$\\label{eq:nested-transfer}\n K=S_{R'}^{-1}S_R\n \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad\n K^*BK=B,\\qquad \\left\\lVert K-I\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert(K-I)P\\right\\rVert_B\\leq Ch\\eta.$$ The construction commutes with orthogonal direct sums and with every orthogonal projection commuting with $A$, $B$, and $P$.",
            "source_id": "PM-251.2"

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-list) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-list): Strong proximality, Hahn-Banach separation, Riesz representation, and rational convex approximation -> Finite group element list with uniform entry frequency above one minus beta
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-flags) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-flags): Backward stage induction over disjoint open boundary subsets and uniform lists -> Multi-stage alphabets and step flags satisfying compatibility and departure-arrival sparsity
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sparse): Haar orthogonal measure concentration, epsilon-nets on sparse spheres, and union bound -> Signed union of two orthonormal bases and anticommuting symmetric involutions with bounded sparse restrictions
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-priority) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-priority): Block LDU Schur complement factorization in the B-Euclidean coordinate decomposition -> Subspace-preserving metric transfer isometry with compressed error on the priority subspace
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-truncate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-truncate): Block matrix restriction to the orthogonal complement and polar decomposition unitary factor -> Modified unitary fixing the projected subspace with dimension-free norm distance
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-comparison) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Arrival frame replacement, cancellation of common exchanges via flag compatibility, and commutation of truncated priority corrections across orthogonal levels -> Dimension-free operator-norm bound between two-configuration and three-configuration circuit comparisons
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transport): Union bound over forward and backward path flag probabilities and telescoping inverse circuit transfers along good paths -> Transport of final-level unit vectors to initial-level unit vectors on all but a 2 N beta fraction of labels
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-witness): Prefix-suffix group product factorization, signed frame trace cancellation, and Jordan-Wigner Clifford trace evaluation -> Vanishing quadratic form on genuine representations and one-fourth lower bound on state vector distance
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-witness-2): Sequential parameter selection balancing defect bound against transport and witness separation bounds, combined with Kazhdan amenability stability -> Equivalence of amenability and strong Ulam stability for countable discrete groups

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **strong-ulam-amenability**: A countable discrete group is strongly Ulam stable if and only if it is amenable; for every nonamenable countable group and every positive defect threshold, there exists a normalized unitary almost representation on a separable Hilbert space that stays bounded away from all genuine unitary representations on the same space.
- **priority-subspace-transfer**: Between two positive metric operators bounded between one half and two whose difference has small compression on a commuting projection subspace, there exists an invertible metric-transfer operator that preserves the priority subspace and keeps its restriction close to the identity.
- **clifford-witness-gap**: On the finite label space equipped with Jordan-Wigner Clifford generators and signed unions of two orthonormal bases, the witness operator has zero trace, squares to the identity, vanishes in expectation on every genuine unitary representation state, and evaluates to one half on the level-zero reference state.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **nonamenable-uniform-repair**: Assume that any unitary almost representation with sufficiently small operator-norm defect can be uniformly corrected to a genuine unitary representation without checking group amenability. -> For every nonamenable countable discrete group and every positive defect bound, there exist normalized unitary almost representations that remain more than one tenth away in operator norm from every genuine unitary representation on the same space.
- **finite-dimensional-surrogate**: Use finite-dimensional unitary stability or finite-dimensional test suites to certify strong Ulam stability across all complex Hilbert spaces. -> Nonamenable groups can satisfy uniform stability when restricted to finite-dimensional unitary groups while failing strong Ulam stability on infinite-dimensional separable Hilbert spaces.
- **naive-layer-error-summation**: Transfer level metrics between successive layers using an arbitrary isometry without preserving the priority departure subspace of the next layer. -> Unprotected metric transfer errors of order h accumulate linearly across N layers, destroying defect control since N scales quadratically with the inverse step size.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. While the boundary action and infinite-dimensional Hilbert space counterexample are non-constructive, the finite-dimensional Jordan-Wigner Clifford operator D, level-zero metric G_0 = I + (1/2)D, and signed two-basis frame cancellation in Lemma 9 are explicit finite matrix constructions. This harness verifies exact signed frame cancellation, anticommutation gamma_d gamma_e + gamma_e gamma_d = 2 delta_{de} I, Tr(D) = 0, and the reference expectation Tr(D G_0)/\|I\| = 1/2 over rational orthonormal frames; it does not decide group amenability or construct the infinite-dimensional almost representation.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON array of cases, each with stages and rational real unit bases.
# PRECONDITIONS: One or two stages, dimension one or two; orthogonal unit bases.
# OUTPUTS: JSON array of exact pairs [trace_D, witness_r] as rational strings.
# TIME: O(c * N^3) exact rational operations for c cases and label count N.
# SPACE: O(c + N^2) exact rational entries excluding input storage.
# ARITHMETIC: Certified exact python-flint fmpq arithmetic; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and exit nonzero; no approximate result.
import json, sys, itertools
from flint import fmpq

cases = json.load(sys.stdin)
assert isinstance(cases, list) and 1 <= len(cases) <= 64
out = []
for case in cases:
    stages = case['stages']
    L = len(stages)
    assert L in (1, 2)
    m_list = []
    frames = []
    signs = []
    J_list = []
    Z_list = []
    for st in stages:
        b_plus = [[fmpq(x) for x in v] for v in st['basis_plus']]
        b_minus = [[fmpq(x) for x in v] for v in st['basis_minus']]
        O_mat = [[fmpq(x) for x in row] for row in st['O_matrix']]
        m = len(b_plus)
        n = len(O_mat)
        assert m in (1, 2) and len(b_minus) == m and n == 1
        for basis in (b_plus, b_minus):
            for i in range(m):
                assert len(basis[i]) == m
                for j in range(m):
                    dot = sum(basis[i][c] * basis[j][c] for c in range(m))
                    assert dot == (1 if i == j else 0)
        assert len(O_mat[0]) == 1 and O_mat[0][0] * O_mat[0][0] == 1
        ov = O_mat[0][0]
        m_list.append(m)
        frames.append(b_plus + b_minus)
        signs.append([fmpq(1)] * m + [fmpq(-1)] * m)
        J_list.append([[fmpq(0), ov], [ov, fmpq(0)]])
        Z_list.append([[fmpq(1), fmpq(0)], [fmpq(0), fmpq(-1)]])
    x_ranges = [range(2 * m) for m in m_list]
    y_ranges = [range(2) for _ in range(L)]
    labels = list(itertools.product(*x_ranges, *y_ranges))
    N = len(labels)
    gammas = []
    for d in range(L):
        mat = [[fmpq(0) for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
        for i, lab_i in enumerate(labels):
            xs_i, ys_i = lab_i[:L], lab_i[L:]
            for j, lab_j in enumerate(labels):
                xs_j, ys_j = lab_j[:L], lab_j[L:]
                if xs_i != xs_j:
                    continue
                prod = signs[d][xs_i[d]]
                for k in range(L):
                    if k < d:
                        prod *= Z_list[k][ys_i[k]][ys_j[k]]
                    elif k == d:
                        prod *= J_list[k][ys_i[k]][ys_j[k]]
                    else:
                        if ys_i[k] != ys_j[k]:
                            prod = fmpq(0)
                mat[i][j] = prod
        gammas.append(mat)
    # Check Jordan-Wigner relations: gamma_d^* = gamma_d, gamma_d^2 = I, and anticommutation
    for d1 in range(L):
        for i in range(N):
            for j in range(N):
                assert gammas[d1][i][j] == gammas[d1][j][i]
        for d2 in range(L):
            for i in range(N):
                for j in range(N):
                    sq_ij = sum(gammas[d1][i][u] * gammas[d2][u][j] + gammas[d2][i][u] * gammas[d1][u][j] for u in range(N))
                    if d1 == d2:
                        assert sq_ij == (2 if i == j else 0)
                    else:
                        assert sq_ij == 0
    tr_d = sum(gammas[d][i][i] for d in range(L) for i in range(N))
    wit_num = sum(gammas[d1][i][j] * gammas[d2][j][i] for d1 in range(L) for d2 in range(L) for i in range(N) for j in range(N))
    val_r = tr_d / N + wit_num / (2 * L * N)
    for d in range(L):
        m = m_list[d]
        cancel = [[fmpq(0) for _ in range(m)] for _ in range(m)]
        for x_idx in range(2 * m):
            vec = frames[d][x_idx]
            sgn = signs[d][x_idx]
            for r_i in range(m):
                for c_i in range(m):
                    cancel[r_i][c_i] += sgn * vec[r_i] * vec[c_i]
        assert all(cancel[r_i][c_i] == 0 for r_i in range(m) for c_i in range(m))
    out.append([str(tr_d), str(val_r)])
print(json.dumps(out))
```

  "panel2": {
    "anti_uses": [
      {
        "evidence": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
            "exact_quote": "A countable discrete group is strongly Ulam stable if and only if it is amenable.",
            "source_object": "Main characterization theorem"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "eq:distance-goal",
            "exact_quote": "More precisely, for every nonamenable countable group and every $\\delta>0$, we construct a separable Hilbert space $H$ and a normalized map $\\mu:G\\to\\mathcal U(H)$ with $\\operatorname{def}(\\mu)<\\delta$ such that $$\\label{eq:distance-goal}\n \\sup_{g\\in G}\\left\\lVert\\mu(g)-\\rho(g)\\right\\rVert>\\frac1{10}\n \\quad\\text{for every unitary representation }\\rho:G\\to\\mathcal U(H).$$",
            "source_object": "Nonamenable counterexample bound"
          }
        ],
        "failure_mode": "For every nonamenable countable discrete group and every positive defect bound, there exist normalized unitary almost representations that remain more than one tenth away in operator norm from every genuine unitary representation on the same space.",
        "id": "nonamenable-uniform-repair",
        "safer_action": "Verify that the discrete group is amenable before invoking uniform operator-norm representation stability on general Hilbert spaces.",
        "tempting_use": "Assume that any unitary almost representation with sufficiently small operator-norm defect can be uniformly corrected to a genuine unitary representation without checking group amenability.",
        "verification": "PENDING",
        "violated_condition": "Strong Ulam stability holds if and only if the countable discrete group is amenable."
      },
      {
        "evidence": [
          {
            "anchor": "pm-h-3",
            "exact_quote": "The inclusion of infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces is essential. The term *Ulam stability*, without “strong”, commonly denotes the uniform statement restricted to finite-dimensional unitary groups",
            "source_object": "Distinction between finite-dimensional and strong Ulam stability"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "pm-h-3",
            "exact_quote": "Nonamenable groups can satisfy that property.",
            "source_object": "Finite-dimensional stability of nonamenable groups"
          }
        ],
        "failure_mode": "Nonamenable groups can satisfy uniform stability when restricted to finite-dimensional unitary groups while failing strong Ulam stability on infinite-dimensional separable Hilbert spaces.",
        "id": "finite-dimensional-surrogate",
        "safer_action": "Distinguish finite-dimensional Ulam stability from strong Ulam stability and never extrapolate finite-dimensional stability certificates to infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces.",
        "tempting_use": "Use finite-dimensional unitary stability or finite-dimensional test suites to certify strong Ulam stability across all complex Hilbert spaces.",
        "verification": "PENDING",
        "violated_condition": "Strong Ulam stability requires a single defect threshold that works simultaneously for all complex Hilbert spaces, including infinite-dimensional ones."
      },
      {
        "evidence": [
          {
            "anchor": "pm-h-4",
            "exact_quote": "Summing the resulting errors over all layers would give no useful bound. We instead choose the isometry between these inner products to be almost the coordinate identity on the subspace used by the next layer.",
            "source_object": "Priority subspace design rationale"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "eq:moved-errors",
            "exact_quote": "\\left\\lVert\\widetilde K_N\\cdots\\widetilde K_1-I\\right\\rVert\n =\\max_k\\left\\lVert\\widetilde K_k-I\\right\\rVert\\le Ch.",
            "source_object": "Orthogonal level maximum bound"
          }
        ],
        "failure_mode": "Unprotected metric transfer errors of order h accumulate linearly across N layers, destroying defect control since N scales quadratically with the inverse step size.",
        "id": "naive-layer-error-summation",
        "safer_action": "Use the Schur-complement priority transfer and polar truncation so that the layer comparison errors commute past subsequent layers and act on orthogonal levels.",
        "tempting_use": "Transfer level metrics between successive layers using an arbitrary isometry without preserving the priority departure subspace of the next layer.",
        "verification": "PENDING",
        "violated_condition": "The metric transfer must fix the next layer's departure subspace up to an error proportional to h times eta so that corrected unitaries act on mutually orthogonal levels and combine by a maximum rather than a sum."
      }
    ],

    "oracle_code": "import json as js\nimport sys as osargs\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\npayload = js.load(open(osargs.argv[1]))\nif not isinstance(payload, list) or not (1 <= len(payload) <= 64):\n    raise ValueError('batch')\ntruth = []\nfor item in payload:\n    st_list = item['stages']\n    depth = len(st_list)\n    if depth not in (1, 2):\n        raise ValueError('depth')\n    for st in st_list:\n        bp = [[Fraction(x) for x in v] for v in st['basis_plus']]\n        bm = [[Fraction(x) for x in v] for v in st['basis_minus']]\n        om = [[Fraction(x) for x in r] for r in st['O_matrix']]\n        dim = len(bp)\n        if dim not in (1, 2) or len(bm) != dim or len(om) != 1 or len(om[0]) != 1:\n            raise ValueError('shape')\n        if om[0][0] ** 2 != 1:\n            raise ValueError('orthogonal_1d')\n        for bset in (bp, bm):\n            for a in range(dim):\n                if len(bset[a]) != dim:\n                    raise ValueError('vec_len')\n                for b in range(dim):\n                    ip = sum(bset[a][k] * bset[b][k] for k in range(dim))\n                    if ip != (1 if a == b else 0):\n                        raise ValueError('orthonormal')\n        for r0 in range(dim):\n            for c0 in range(dim):\n                s_plus = sum(v[r0] * v[c0] for v in bp)\n                s_minus = sum(v[r0] * v[c0] for v in bm)\n                if s_plus - s_minus != 0:\n                    raise ValueError('frame_cancel')\n    truth.append(['0', '1/2'])\ntry:\n    produced = js.load(open(osargs.argv[2]))\n    supplied = js.loads(osargs.argv[3])\nexcept Exception:\n    raise SystemExit(9)\nraise SystemExit(0 if produced == truth and supplied == truth else 7)\n",
    "output_contract": "JSON list of [Tr(D), <r, (D otimes I) r>] pairs as exact rational strings (['0', '1/2'] for every valid configuration).",
    "reason": "While the boundary action and infinite-dimensional Hilbert space counterexample are non-constructive, the finite-dimensional Jordan-Wigner Clifford operator D, level-zero metric G_0 = I + (1/2)D, and signed two-basis frame cancellation in Lemma 9 are explicit finite matrix constructions. This harness verifies exact signed frame cancellation, anticommutation gamma_d gamma_e + gamma_e gamma_d = 2 delta_{de} I, Tr(D) = 0, and the reference expectation Tr(D G_0)/|I| = 1/2 over rational orthonormal frames; it does not decide group amenability or construct the infinite-dimensional almost representation.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON array of cases, each with stages and rational real unit bases.\n# PRECONDITIONS: One or two stages, dimension one or two; orthogonal unit bases.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON array of exact pairs [trace_D, witness_r] as rational strings.\n# TIME: O(c * N^3) exact rational operations for c cases and label count N.\n# SPACE: O(c + N^2) exact rational entries excluding input storage.\n# ARITHMETIC: Certified exact python-flint fmpq arithmetic; no floating point.\n# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and exit nonzero; no approximate result.\nimport json, sys, itertools\nfrom flint import fmpq\n\ncases = json.load(sys.stdin)\nassert isinstance(cases, list) and 1 <= len(cases) <= 64\nout = []\nfor case in cases:\n    stages = case['stages']\n    L = len(stages)\n    assert L in (1, 2)\n    m_list = []\n    frames = []\n    signs = []\n    J_list = []\n    Z_list = []\n    for st in stages:\n        b_plus = [[fmpq(x) for x in v] for v in st['basis_plus']]\n        b_minus = [[fmpq(x) for x in v] for v in st['basis_minus']]\n        O_mat = [[fmpq(x) for x in row] for row in st['O_matrix']]\n        m = len(b_plus)\n        n = len(O_mat)\n        assert m in (1, 2) and len(b_minus) == m and n == 1\n        for basis in (b_plus, b_minus):\n            for i in range(m):\n                assert len(basis[i]) == m\n                for j in range(m):\n                    dot = sum(basis[i][c] * basis[j][c] for c in range(m))\n                    assert dot == (1 if i == j else 0)\n        assert len(O_mat[0]) == 1 and O_mat[0][0] * O_mat[0][0] == 1\n        ov = O_mat[0][0]\n        m_list.append(m)\n        frames.append(b_plus + b_minus)\n        signs.append([fmpq(1)] * m + [fmpq(-1)] * m)\n        J_list.append([[fmpq(0), ov], [ov, fmpq(0)]])\n        Z_list.append([[fmpq(1), fmpq(0)], [fmpq(0), fmpq(-1)]])\n    x_ranges = [range(2 * m) for m in m_list]\n    y_ranges = [range(2) for _ in range(L)]\n    labels = list(itertools.product(*x_ranges, *y_ranges))\n    N = len(labels)\n    gammas = []\n    for d in range(L):\n        mat = [[fmpq(0) for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]\n        for i, lab_i in enumerate(labels):\n            xs_i, ys_i = lab_i[:L], lab_i[L:]\n            for j, lab_j in enumerate(labels):\n                xs_j, ys_j = lab_j[:L], lab_j[L:]\n                if xs_i != xs_j:\n                    continue\n                prod = signs[d][xs_i[d]]\n                for k in range(L):\n                    if k < d:\n                        prod *= Z_list[k][ys_i[k]][ys_j[k]]\n                    elif k == d:\n                        prod *= J_list[k][ys_i[k]][ys_j[k]]\n                    else:\n                        if ys_i[k] != ys_j[k]:\n                            prod = fmpq(0)\n                mat[i][j] = prod\n        gammas.append(mat)\n    # Check Jordan-Wigner relations: gamma_d^* = gamma_d, gamma_d^2 = I, and anticommutation\n    for d1 in range(L):\n        for i in range(N):\n            for j in range(N):\n                assert gammas[d1][i][j] == gammas[d1][j][i]\n        for d2 in range(L):\n            for i in range(N):\n                for j in range(N):\n                    sq_ij = sum(gammas[d1][i][u] * gammas[d2][u][j] + gammas[d2][i][u] * gammas[d1][u][j] for u in range(N))\n                    if d1 == d2:\n                        assert sq_ij == (2 if i == j else 0)\n                    else:\n                        assert sq_ij == 0\n    tr_d = sum(gammas[d][i][i] for d in range(L) for i in range(N))\n    wit_num = sum(gammas[d1][i][j] * gammas[d2][j][i] for d1 in range(L) for d2 in range(L) for i in range(N) for j in range(N))\n    val_r = tr_d / N + wit_num / (2 * L * N)\n    for d in range(L):\n        m = m_list[d]\n        cancel = [[fmpq(0) for _ in range(m)] for _ in range(m)]\n        for x_idx in range(2 * m):\n            vec = frames[d][x_idx]\n            sgn = signs[d][x_idx]\n            for r_i in range(m):\n                for c_i in range(m):\n                    cancel[r_i][c_i] += sgn * vec[r_i] * vec[c_i]\n        assert all(cancel[r_i][c_i] == 0 for r_i in range(m) for c_i in range(m))\n    out.append([str(tr_d), str(val_r)])\nprint(json.dumps(out))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper establishes a mathematical equivalence between group amenability and strong Ulam stability over arbitrary complex Hilbert spaces. A software implementation, repair routine, finite-precision error bound, or software execution contract is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No game mechanic, interactive strategic model, or numerical simulation procedure is studied. The labelled paths and stage transitions are mathematical proof devices derived from a compact Hausdorff boundary action; a simulation model is ABSENT. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper provides no solver, automated verifier, or computer-algebra tool. The characterization and counterexample rely on non-constructive steps including Furstenberg boundaries and Hahn-Banach separation; an executable tool specification is ABSENT. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Although Lemma 5 and Lemma 6 give explicit operator formulas via Schur complements and polar factors, the overall construction of the unstable almost representation is non-constructive: it invokes Furstenberg boundary existence, Hahn-Banach separation, and probabilistic Haar-orthogonal existence, and acts on an infinite direct sum over the group. A finite terminating algorithm and complexity analysis are ABSENT. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The multi-level coordinate spaces, label product sets, and Hilbert space direct sums over group elements are mathematical tensor and direct-sum structures used in an existence proof. Memory layouts, container operations, and storage bounds are ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Although the proof uses the terminology of unitary circuits and Jordan-Wigner parity strings on complexified coordinate spaces, these are abstract operator-theoretic constructions on an infinite direct sum over a discrete group. Physical quantum or classical hardware architectures, gate synthesis, and hardware resource models are ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No cryptographic primitive, threat model, hardness assumption, or security protocol appears in the paper. A cryptographic or security contract is ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper develops no statistical learning model, training objective, generalization bound, or neural architecture. A machine-learning contract is ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Hahn-Banach separation and quadratic-form expectation bounds are used exclusively for mathematical existence and obstruction arguments. Finite optimization formulations, solvers, and operations-research models are ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No distributed consensus, message passing, replication, or fault-tolerance protocol is studied. Group locations index orthogonal summands of a single Hilbert space; a distributed-systems contract is ABSENT. |
| other (functional analysis, operator algebras, and unitary representation theory of discrete groups) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The paper resolves the question of Burger, Ozawa, and Thom by proving that a countable discrete group is strongly Ulam stable if and only if it is amenable, and constructs for every nonamenable countable discrete group and every positive defect tolerance a separable Hilbert space and a normalized unitary almost representation whose uniform operator-norm distance to every genuine unitary representation exceeds one tenth. It also supplies dimension-free linear-algebraic lemmas for sparse frame restrictions, subspace-preserving metric transfers, and exact subspace fixing of unitaries. This is a mathematical theory and proof-architecture contract; an executable software implementation is ABSENT. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The strong Ulam stability characterization applies to countable discrete groups and requires that the stability threshold works simultaneously across all complex Hilbert spaces, including infinite-dimensional ones. Blocked use: ['Restricting stability tests to finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces or non-discrete topological groups without establishing infinite-dimensional uniform bounds.']
- **L02** (formal_scope): Finite-dimensional Ulam stability is a distinct property from strong Ulam stability; nonamenable groups can satisfy finite-dimensional Ulam stability while failing strong Ulam stability on infinite-dimensional spaces. Blocked use: ['Using finite-dimensional unitary representation stability or finite-dimensional matrix tests to certify strong Ulam stability or group amenability.']
- **L03** (hypothesis): Both the multiplicativity defect and the distance to genuine unitary representations are measured uniformly over the entire group in the operator norm on the same Hilbert space, for normalized maps sending the group identity to the identity operator. Blocked use: ['Substituting pointwise convergence, average error over finite generating sets, Hilbert-Schmidt or Frobenius norms, or representations acting on a modified Hilbert space.']
- **L04** (counterexample_scope): For any nonamenable countable discrete group and any positive defect threshold, there exists a normalized unitary almost representation on a separable Hilbert space whose uniform operator-norm distance to every genuine unitary representation exceeds 1/10. Blocked use: ['Assuming approximate unitary homomorphisms of nonamenable groups can be uniformly repaired or projected onto genuine unitary representations when the defect is sufficiently small.']
- **L05** (nonconstructive): The construction of the multi-stage step lists and compatible sparse flags relies on a compact Hausdorff Furstenberg boundary, weak-star orbit closures of Radon probability measures, and Hahn-Banach separation. Blocked use: ['Treating the flag construction as an explicit finite-time combinatorial algorithm without an oracle for the boundary action and separating measures.']
- **L06** (nonconstructive): The signed orthonormal bases and anticommuting involutions with sparse restriction bounds are obtained nonconstructively via measure concentration on the Haar-distributed orthogonal group for sufficiently large alphabet dimensions. Blocked use: ['Using arbitrary small dimensions or unverified deterministic matrices without certifying the sparse submatrix operator-norm bound across allsubsets of size up to 4\\beta\|X\|.']
- **L07** (hypothesis): The subspace-preserving metric transfer lemma requires positive operators bounded between 1/2 I and 2 I, a self-adjoint difference of norm at most h <= 1/8, an orthogonal projection commuting with B, and a compressed difference norm at most eta <= 1. Blocked use: ['Applying the priority isometry bounds when the priority projection fails to commute with the target metric B, when metrics approach singularity outside [1/2 I, 2 I], or when the perturbation step h exceeds 1/8.']
- **L08** (hypothesis): Exact subspace-fixing unitary truncation requires a unitary operator within distance t < 1/2 of the identity and subspace restriction error e <= 1/2 so that the diagonal compression remains invertible. Blocked use: ['Applying polar-factor subspace truncation to operators with large global deviation t >= 1/2 where the compressed block on the orthogonal complement may fail to be invertible.']
- **L09** (edge_case): The unitary exchange operator on a switchable edge uses the priority transfer inverse on the left-to-right block and the fixed transfer on the right-to-left block, so the exchange is unitary but need not be an involution. Blocked use: ['Assuming edge-exchange layers are self-adjoint involutions or replacing the asymmetric off-diagonal blocks with symmetric transfers.']
- **L10** (approximation): The circuit comparison bound, the almost-representation defect bound, and the path transport bound depend on unspecified absolute numerical constants C and h_0 > 0, and require sequential parameter selection where L (and N = L + 1) is fixed before eta, which is fixed before beta and the alphabet sizes. Blocked use: ['Choosing the parameters h, eta, and beta independently or reversing their dependency order, or treating the asymptotic bounds as explicit numerical thresholds without bounding C.']
- **L11** (formal_scope): Finite-dimensional linear algebra checks of Lemma 5 (metric transfer), Lemma 6 (unitary truncation), Jordan-Wigner Clifford identities, and Lemma 9 (quadratic-form witness) verify those local matrix steps only and cannot certify the infinite-dimensional nonamenable group construction. Blocked use: ['Claiming that finite matrix oracle verification of the linear-algebra lemmas certifies Theorem 1 or replaces the infinite direct sum over the group.']
- **L12** (unknown): Explicit numerical values for the absolute constants C and h_0, effective dimension thresholds for the Haar orthogonal concentration in Lemma 4, and constructive correction algorithms for amenable almost representations are ABSENT from the paper. Blocked use: ['Deploying the paper as a concrete numerical algorithm for repairing approximate representations or computing explicit defect thresholds.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


A countable discrete group is amenable if and only if it is strongly Ulam stable: every sufficiently accurate unitary almost representation, on any complex Hilbert space, is uniformly close in operator norm to a genuine representation on the same space. We prove the converse to Kazhdan's amenable stability theorem, answering the question of Burger, Ozawa, and Thom. The inclusion of infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces is essential.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
